2. Where Gateway fits in AgentCore #

ServiceResponsibilityRelationship to Gateway
GatewayUnified, governed entry point to tools, agents and models—
RuntimeServerless, session-isolated agent hostingAgents on Runtime are Gateway clients; Runtime agents can also be Gateway targets (GA Apr 2026)
IdentityWorkload identity, token vault, OAuth/API-key providersSupplies every outbound credential Gateway injects — the two are inseparable in practice
PolicyCedar-based authorization engineAttached to a gateway to authorise individual tool calls, with Guardrails integration
MemoryShort/long-term agent memoryIndependent, though Memory is reachable as an HTTP connector target
ObservabilityCloudWatch GenAI traces and metricsGateway emits invocation, latency and target-execution metrics plus vended logs
Identity is not optional in practice

Any target using API-key or OAuth outbound auth needs a credential provider in the AgentCore Identity token vault. The good news: Identity is free when used through Gateway or Runtime — the $0.010 per 1,000 token requests applies only to standalone use.

3. When to use it #

DimensionAgentCore GatewayFramework-native toolsSelf-hosted MCP serverDirect API calls from agent
Setup effortConfig only, zero tool codeLowest for a handful of toolsYou write and operate a serverLowest, until it isn't
Reuse across agentsNative — one endpoint, many clientsNone; each agent re-implementsYes, if you operate itNone
Credential isolationAgent never holds target credentialsAgent holds themServer holds themAgent holds them
Tool discovery at scaleSemantic search built inManualYou build itn/a
Central governanceCedar policy, rate limits, interceptors, auditNoneYou build itNone
LatencyOne managed hop (+ interceptors)DirectOne self-managed hopDirect
Ops burdenVery lowNoneHighNone

3.1 Good fits

  • Several agents needing the same tools, or an organisation standardising tool access.
  • Existing REST APIs or Lambda functions you want agent-accessible without writing MCP servers.
  • Credentials that must not be handed to agent code (finance, HR, customer systems).
  • Large tool catalogues where discovery, not execution, is the bottleneck.
  • Anywhere you need a single audited chokepoint for “what did the agents actually call?”
  • Multi-provider LLM access behind one endpoint with consistent governance (inference targets).

3.2 Poor fits

  • A single agent with two or three tools — framework-native function calling wins on simplicity.
  • Latency-critical inner loops where an extra managed hop matters.
  • Bulk data transfer: tool payloads cap at 6 MB and Smithy streaming operations are unsupported.
  • Tools whose contract changes hourly — every change means re-sync or re-import.

4. Core concepts #

ConceptDefinitionNotes
GatewayThe managed endpoint agents connect to. Owns inbound auth, KMS key, service role, protocol config, policy engine and interceptors.Name pattern ([0-9a-zA-Z][-]?){1,48}. URL: https://{gatewayId}.gateway.bedrock-agentcore.{region}.amazonaws.com
TargetA backend the gateway connects to, in one of three categories: MCP, HTTP or inference.100 per gateway (adjustable). Each carries its own outbound credential configuration.
ToolA single callable operation exposed over MCP, named {targetName}___{toolName}.1,000 per target; tool name up to 256 chars.
AuthorizerInbound authorization config: CUSTOM_JWT, AWS_IAM, AUTHENTICATE_ONLY or NONE.Set at creation; governs who may reach the gateway at all.
Credential providerOutbound credential for a target — gateway IAM role, API key, or OAuth (2LO/3LO/token exchange).Stored in the AgentCore Identity token vault. One config per target.
Workload identityThe gateway's own identity, returned as workloadIdentityDetails at creation.Used for token exchange against the vault.
Policy engineCedar authorization attached in ENFORCE or AUDIT mode.Default-deny under ENFORCE.
InterceptorLambda invoked at the REQUEST and/or RESPONSE interception point.Max one of each per gateway.
Listing modeDEFAULT (cached catalogue, explicit sync) or DYNAMIC (discovered at runtime).DYNAMIC is incompatible with semantic search and 3LO.

5. The three target categories #

MCP targetsHTTP targetsInference targets
Gateway behaviourAggregation — acts as one virtual MCP server combining every target's capabilitiesPassthrough — proxies requests directly, no translationModel routing — routes LLM traffic by model ID
ProtocolMCP (JSON-RPC over streamable HTTP)Whatever the backend speaksOpenAI / Anthropic / Bedrock APIs
BackendsLambda, API Gateway REST stages, OpenAPI, Smithy, MCP servers, integrations, connectorsAgentCore Runtime agents, A2A services, any HTTP endpointBedrock, OpenAI, Anthropic, OpenAI-compatible services
Tool discoverytools/list + semantic searchn/aAggregated model listing
Typical useTurning enterprise assets into agent toolsFronting agents/services without changing their protocolOne endpoint and one governance point across providers
Why this split matters architecturally

Only MCP targets get aggregation, tool naming, semantic search and per-tool Cedar actions. Choosing an HTTP passthrough target because it was easier to configure quietly forfeits all four. Decide by what governance you need, not by what imports fastest.

6. Lambda targets #

The most flexible MCP target: you supply a tool schema and a function, and the gateway handles MCP entirely.

6.1 Tool schema

json
{
  "name": "get_weather",
  "description": "Get weather for a location",
  "inputSchema": {
    "type": "object",
    "properties": {
      "location": { "type": "string", "description": "the location e.g. seattle, wa" }
    },
    "required": ["location"]
  },
  "outputSchema": {
    "type": "object",
    "properties": {
      "temperature": { "type": "number" },
      "conditions":  { "type": "string" }
    }
  }
}

name, description and inputSchema are required; outputSchema is optional but improves how models reason about results.

6.2 What the Lambda receives

The event is a flat map of your inputSchema properties to values. The context carries gateway metadata under client_context.custom:

KeyContents
bedrockAgentCoreToolName{target_name}___{tool_name} — prefixed
bedrockAgentCoreMessageVersionPayload version, currently 1.0
bedrockAgentCoreAwsRequestIdRequest correlation ID
bedrockAgentCoreMcpMessageIdMCP JSON-RPC message ID
bedrockAgentCoreGatewayId / bedrockAgentCoreTargetIdOriginating gateway and target
python
DELIM = "___"

def lambda_handler(event, context):
    raw = context.client_context.custom["bedrockAgentCoreToolName"]
    tool_name = raw[raw.index(DELIM) + len(DELIM):]      # strip the target prefix

    if tool_name == "searchProducts":
        return search_products(event.get("keywords"), event.get("category"))
    if tool_name == "getProductDetails":
        return get_product_details(event.get("product_id"))
    return {"error": f"Unknown tool: {tool_name}"}


def search_products(keywords, category):
    return {"results": [{"id": "123", "name": "Wireless Headphones", "price": 99.99}]}
The classic bug

Forgetting to strip ___. Your router compares "myTarget___searchProducts" == "searchProducts", never matches, and every call returns “unknown tool.” Write the strip first, before any business logic.

Gateway needs lambda:InvokeFunction on the function, the response must be valid JSON, and the function timeout should sit comfortably inside the gateway's 15-minute invocation ceiling.

7. OpenAPI targets #

AspectDetail
Supported versionsOpenAPI 3.0 and 3.1. Swagger 2.0 not supported
DeliveryInline (≤1 MB) or from S3 (≤10 MB)
Tool nameThe operation's operationId — required on every exposed operation
SupportedPrimitive and complex types, nested objects, arrays, standard HTTP methods; application/json, application/xml, multipart/form-data, application/x-www-form-urlencoded
Not supportedoneOf, anyOf, allOf; complex parameter serializers; spec-level security schemes (configure auth on the gateway target instead)
Outbound authAPI key (header or query), OAuth 2LO/3LO, IAM SigV4, or none
json
{
  "openapi": "3.0.0",
  "info": { "title": "Weather API", "version": "1.0.0" },
  "servers": [ { "url": "https://api.example.com/v1" } ],
  "paths": {
    "/weather": {
      "get": {
        "operationId": "getCurrentWeather",
        "summary": "Get current weather",
        "description": "Returns current weather information for a location",
        "parameters": [
          { "name": "location", "in": "query", "required": true,
            "description": "City name or coordinates",
            "schema": { "type": "string" } },
          { "name": "units", "in": "query", "required": false,
            "schema": { "type": "string", "enum": ["metric", "imperial"], "default": "metric" } }
        ],
        "responses": {
          "200": { "description": "Successful response",
            "content": { "application/json": { "schema": {
              "type": "object",
              "properties": { "temperature": {"type": "number"}, "conditions": {"type": "string"} }
            } } } }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
Your spec is now agent-facing product surface

operationId becomes the tool name and description becomes the model's decision criterion. A spec written for human SDK generators — terse descriptions, getV2ResourceById naming — will produce an agent that picks the wrong tool. Budget a remediation pass: rename operations to intent-revealing verbs, write descriptions that say when to use this, and describe every parameter.

8. Smithy targets #

Smithy models expose AWS services as tools. Gateway ships built-in models for common AWS services (see the AWS API Models repository).

SupportedUnsupported
Service, operation and resource definitionsRestXml, JsonRpc, AwsQuery, Ec2Query protocols
JSON protocol bindings and restJson1Streaming operations
Primitive and complex types (structures, lists, maps)Multiple egress authentication types on one target
HTTP bindings — path, query and header parametersCustom protocol implementations
Endpoint rules with region validationCustom (non-AWS) Smithy models

Maximum model size is 10 MB; models may be inline or in S3. Outbound auth is normally GATEWAY_IAM_ROLE with SigV4.

Endpoint templating is an SSRF surface

Avoid unconstrained placeholders such as https://{host}/api/v1 or https://{subdomain}.{env}.{domain}.com — a model-supplied value could redirect calls anywhere. Prefer static fully-qualified URLs, or constrain dynamic parameters to a validated set. For AWS services, rely on standard endpoint resolution with validated region parameters.

9. MCP server targets #

Point the gateway at an existing MCP server and its tools, prompts and resources are folded into the aggregate catalogue.

9.1 Requirements

  • The server must expose tool capabilities; prompts and resources are optional.
  • Supported MCP protocol versions: 2026-07-28, 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26.
  • The server URL must be URL-encoded; the gateway uses the same URL to invoke it.

9.2 Listing modes

ModeBehaviourTrade-off
DEFAULTCapabilities fetched and cached at target create/update; refreshed explicitly via SynchronizeGatewayTargets. Embeddings pre-computed for search.Fast, searchable, but can go stale — you own the refresh.
DYNAMICCapabilities discovered at runtime on invocation.Always fresh, but incompatible with semantic search and 3LO.

9.3 Synchronisation

Implicit sync happens during CreateGatewayTarget/UpdateGatewayTarget: the gateway calls tools/list, prompts/list, resources/list and resources/templates/list, then pre-computes embeddings. Explicit sync is a separate call:

http
PUT /gateways/{gatewayIdentifier}/synchronize

→ 202 Accepted, processed asynchronously (may take several minutes)
   poll GetGatewayTarget for progress
Static schemas block sync

If you supplied tools upfront via mcpToolSchema — the usual workaround when a 3LO consent flow can't be completed at create time — the gateway parses and caches those definitions without ever calling the server, and you cannot synchronize that target until the static schema is removed. Choose deliberately between “fast to stand up” and “stays current.”

9.4 Outbound auth for MCP servers

MethodNotes
OAuthCLIENT_CREDENTIALS (2LO), AUTHORIZATION_CODE (3LO), TOKEN_EXCHANGE (on-behalf-of)
IAM (SigV4)Uses the gateway service role. Works with AgentCore Gateway, AgentCore Runtime, Amazon API Gateway and Lambda Function URLs. Not with ALB or direct EC2 endpoints.
API keyVia an AgentCore Identity API-key credential provider
NoneNot recommended

For 3LO, an admin completes the consent flow during target creation using an authorization URL returned in the API response. URL session binding verifies that the initiator of the authorization request matches the grantor of consent, and both the OAuth URL and session URI expire after 10 minutes — so a shared link cannot be used to mint tokens.

Latency tip

For MCP servers hosted on AgentCore Runtime, enable MCP sessions by adding Mcp-Session-Id to the allowed request/response headers in the target's metadataConfiguration. This avoids re-initialising the server on every request. Applies to protocol version 2025-11-25 and earlier; 2026-07-28 is stateless.

10. Integrations & connectors #

10.1 Built-in integration templates

Pre-configured target templates for common SaaS and AWS services:

Amazon Bedrock RuntimeCloudWatchDynamoDB AsanaBambooHRBrave Search Coinbase x402 BazaarConfluenceJira Microsoft ExchangeOneDriveSharePointTeams PagerDutySalesforceServiceNow SlackSmartsheetTavily Search ZendeskZoom

IntegrationAuthServer URL
SalesforceOAuth2https://{domainName}.develop.my.salesforce.com/services/data/v60.0
ZendeskOAuth2https://{customerInstanceId}.zendesk.com
BambooHROAuth2https://api.bamboohr.com/api/gateway.php/{companyDomain}
SlackAPI keyhttps://slack.com/api
JiraAPI keyhttps://api.atlassian.com/ex/jira/{customerInstanceId}
ConfluenceAPI keyhttps://{sub-domain}.atlassian.net
ServiceNowAPI keyhttps://{instance}.service-now.com
Microsoft 365 (Exchange/OneDrive/SharePoint/Teams)API keyhttps://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0
PagerDuty / Smartsheet / Zoom / Asana / Tavily / BraveAPI keyProvider endpoints
Coinbase x402 BazaarNonehttps://api.cdp.coinbase.com/platform/v2/x402/discovery/mcp
Console-only — this breaks IaC

Integration provider templates can only be added through the AWS Management Console, not via API or CloudFormation. If reproducible environments matter, model these targets as OpenAPI or MCP-server targets instead and keep them in source control. Also note that only a subset of each provider's API is exposed — send tools/list after setup to see exactly what you got — and where API paths contain variables (e.g. {task_gid}) you pass them in the params field of the JSON-RPC request.

10.2 Web Search connector

A fully managed, MCP-compliant web search tool backed by an Amazon-operated index spanning tens of billions of documents, with a knowledge graph and semantic snippet extraction. Queries never leave AWS infrastructure. Create a gateway target with connectorId: "web-search".

json
{
  "query": "python 3.13 release date",          // required, ≤ 200 characters
  "maxResults": 10,                              // 1–25, default 10
  "filters": {                                   // connector v1.2.0+
    "domainFilter": {
      "include": ["python.org"],                 // up to 100 domains
      "exclude": ["example-spam.com"]
    },
    "publishedDateFilter": {
      "from": "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z",
      "to":   "2026-08-18T00:00:00Z"
    }
  }
}

Filtering is two-layer: administrators set static include/exclude lists at the target level (invisible to agents), and agents may add request-level filters that compose with AND — request filters can never widen the administrator's restrictions. Results carry text, url, title and publishedDate.

Cost and terms

$7 per 1,000 queries — roughly 1,400× a normal gateway tool invocation, and the request rate is capped at 10 TPS. Gate it behind policy or rate limits. Acceptable-use terms also require you to retain and display source citations and links, and forbid bulk extraction or building competing indexes. Available in us-east-1, eu-west-1 and ap-northeast-1.

11. HTTP passthrough targets #

Introduced April 2026. The gateway forwards traffic to an HTTP endpoint without protocol translation or aggregation — useful for fronting AgentCore Runtime agents, A2A services, or any HTTP backend while still getting inbound auth, policy, rate limiting and observability.

CapabilityMCP targetHTTP passthrough target
Protocol translationYes (MCP ⇄ backend)No
Tool aggregation & ___ namingYesNo
Semantic searchYesNo
Policy enforcementPer toolVia supplied API schema
Session stickinessVia MCP session IDConfigurable
Auth optionsFull outbound matrixAdds caller IAM credentials (FAS) and token passthrough

Two auth modes are unique to HTTP targets fronting AgentCore Runtime: caller IAM credentials (forward-access-session, so the target sees the original caller's identity) and token passthrough (the inbound bearer token is forwarded unchanged). Both preserve end-user identity end-to-end — powerful, and worth reviewing carefully, since the downstream service now receives a live credential.

12. Inference targets #

Route LLM traffic through the gateway to multiple providers behind one endpoint, with credentials abstracted away from clients and Guardrails/Policy applied consistently.

TypeUse when
Inference connector targetsZero configuration for supported providers. The recommended default.
Inference provider targetsYou need explicit control of endpoints, model mappings, operations or path rewriting.

12.1 Connector configuration

connectorIdProviderCredential
bedrock-mantleAmazon BedrockGATEWAY_IAM_ROLE
openaiOpenAIAPI key, header Authorization, prefix Bearer
anthropicAnthropicAPI key, header x-api-key
json
{
  "inference": { "connector": { "source": { "connectorId": "openai" } } },
  "credentialProviderConfigurations": [
    {
      "credentialProviderType": "API_KEY",
      "credentialProvider": {
        "apiKeyCredentialProvider": {
          "providerArn": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-west-2:111122223333:token-vault/default/apikeycredentialprovider/openai-key",
          "credentialLocation": "HEADER",
          "credentialParameterName": "Authorization",
          "credentialPrefix": "Bearer "
        }
      }
    }
  ]
}

12.2 Model routing

Clients specify qualified model IDs — {targetName}/{modelId}, e.g. openai/gpt-5.5 — which route deterministically. Unqualified IDs are matched across all targets, with exact matches beating glob patterns; on a collision the Bedrock target wins, otherwise the choice is arbitrary.

Always qualify in production

Unqualified routing is convenient in a demo and non-deterministic in production. A model name that later exists on two providers will start splitting traffic unpredictably. Use {targetName}/{modelId} everywhere.

python
from openai import OpenAI

client = OpenAI(
    base_url="https://GATEWAY_ID.gateway.bedrock-agentcore.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/inference/v1",
    api_key="GATEWAY_AUTH_TOKEN",
)

resp = client.chat.completions.create(
    model="openai/gpt-5.5",
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello!"}],
)

Connectors also strip provider model-ID prefixes, rewrite paths to each provider's API, and expose the standard operations (chat completions, messages). Both the OpenAI and Anthropic SDKs work unmodified against the gateway base URL.

13. Tool naming #

text
${target_name}___${tool_name}

  target:  LambdaUsingSDK
  tool:    get_order_tool
  exposed: LambdaUsingSDK___get_order_tool
                          ▲
                          └── three underscores, fixed, not configurable

The prefix lets two targets expose the same tool name without collision. It also means the target name leaks into every surface that touches tools:

  • Lambda handlers must strip it before routing (§6.2).
  • Cedar policies reference the full prefixed name as the action: AgentCore::Action::"RefundTarget___process_refund".
  • Rate-limit scoping by toolName uses it.
  • Model prompts see it — which means the target name is part of how the model interprets a tool.
Treat target names as public API

Renaming a target changes every tool name it exposes, breaking Lambda routing, Cedar policies, rate-limit entries and any agent prompt that mentions a tool by name. There is no alias mechanism. Choose short, stable, meaningful names — orders, not lambda-target-v2-test.

14. Semantic tool search #

Agents that see hundreds of tools pick badly and burn context. Gateway's answer is a built-in MCP tool, x_amz_bedrock_agentcore_search, that takes a natural-language query and returns the relevant subset.

Enable it at creation

searchType lives in protocolConfiguration.mcpConfiguration and cannot be added to an existing gateway. Retrofitting means creating a new gateway and repointing every client. If there is any chance your catalogue grows, turn it on now.

json
{
  "jsonrpc": "2.0",
  "id": "search-tools-request",
  "method": "tools/call",
  "params": {
    "name": "x_amz_bedrock_agentcore_search",
    "arguments": { "query": "find order information" }
  }
}

Under MCP 2026-07-28, add protocol metadata:

json
"_meta": {
  "io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion": "2026-07-28",
  "io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo": { "name": "my-agent", "version": "1.0.0" },
  "io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities": {}
}
python
from strands.tools.mcp.mcp_client import MCPClient
from mcp.client.streamable_http import streamablehttp_client

mcp_client = MCPClient(lambda: streamablehttp_client(
    GATEWAY_URL, headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {access_token}"}))

with mcp_client:
    result = mcp_client.call_tool_sync(
        tool_use_id="tool-123",
        name="x_amz_bedrock_agentcore_search",
        arguments={"query": "find order information"},
    )

14.1 Using it well

  • Phrase queries conversationally — “find order information” beats “order”.
  • Search once, then dispatch. The 25-per-minute quota and 5× price make per-turn searching untenable.
  • Check supportedVersions covers the MCP protocol version your client sends; adjust with UpdateGateway.
  • Paginate when listing all tools — large catalogues return pagination tokens.

15. Inbound authentication #

authorizerTypeBehaviourUse when
CUSTOM_JWTGateway acts as an OAuth resource server, validating JWTs against your IdP (Cognito, Okta, Auth0, Entra ID). Supports authorization code (3LO) and client credentials (2LO) flows, with allowed audiences and client IDs.End users or third-party agents authenticate to the gateway
AWS_IAMSigV4 request signing; access controlled by bedrock-agentcore:InvokeGateway in IAM.Callers are AWS principals — Runtime agents, Lambda, backend services
AUTHENTICATE_ONLYGateway authenticates the caller but delegates authorization to the target.The backend already owns fine-grained authorization
NONENo inbound authorization.Local development only
json
{
  "authorizerType": "CUSTOM_JWT",
  "authorizerConfiguration": {
    "customJwtConfiguration": {
      "issuer": "https://cognito-idp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/us-east-1_ABC123",
      "audience": ["3n4b5c6d7e8f9g0h1i2j3k4l5m"]
    }
  }
}

Since December 2025 custom claim values are supported in gateway authentication, and since April 2026 you can enforce that traffic originates from a gateway — via aws:SourceArn for IAM/SigV4 targets, or allowedWorkloadConfiguration for OAuth/JWT targets. That closes the bypass where a caller who learns a backend URL skips the gateway entirely.

Do this

Configuring the gateway is only half the job. Lock the backend so it accepts requests only from the gateway. Otherwise your policy engine, rate limits and audit trail are decorative — anyone with the target URL routes around them.

16. Outbound authorization #

Each target carries its own credentialProviderConfigurations, entirely independent of how the caller authenticated inbound.

TypeApplies toNotes
GATEWAY_IAM_ROLELambda, API Gateway, MCP server, OpenAPI, Smithy, AgentCore RuntimeSigV4 with the gateway service role
Caller IAM credentialsAgentCore Runtime (HTTP)Forward access session — target sees the original caller
OAUTHMCP server, OpenAPI, AgentCore Runtime2LO client credentials, 3LO authorization code, or token exchange
API_KEYAPI Gateway, MCP server, OpenAPIPlaced in HEADER or QUERY, with optional prefix
Token passthroughAgentCore Runtime (HTTP)Inbound token forwarded unchanged
NoneAPI Gateway, MCP server, OpenAPI, SmithyNot recommended
json
// IAM (Lambda / API Gateway / Smithy)
{ "credentialProviderType": "GATEWAY_IAM_ROLE" }

// IAM with explicit service (MCP server / OpenAPI)
{ "credentialProviderType": "GATEWAY_IAM_ROLE",
  "iamCredentialProvider": { "service": "bedrock-agentcore", "region": "us-east-1" } }

// OAuth 2LO
{ "credentialProviderType": "OAUTH",
  "oauth2CredentialProvider": {
    "credentialProviderArn": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-east-1:123456789012:token-vault/TokenVaultId/oauth2credentialprovider/CredentialName" } }

// OAuth 3LO
{ "credentialProviderType": "OAUTH",
  "oauth2CredentialProvider": {
    "credentialProviderArn": "...",
    "authorizationCodeFlow": { "redirectUri": "https://your-redirect-uri.example.com" } } }

// OAuth token exchange (on-behalf-of)
{ "credentialProviderType": "OAUTH",
  "oauth2CredentialProvider": {
    "credentialProviderArn": "...",
    "tokenExchangeGrant": { "audience": "downstream-service" } } }

// API key
{ "credentialProviderType": "API_KEY",
  "apiKeyCredentialProvider": {
    "credentialProviderArn": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-east-1:123456789012:token-vault/TokenVaultId/apikeycredentialprovider/Name",
    "credentialLocation": "HEADER",
    "credentialPrefix": "Bearer" } }

16.1 Creating credential providers

bash
# OAuth
agentcore add credential --name oauth-credential-provider --type oauth \
  --discovery-url DISCOVERY_URL --client-id CLIENT_ID --client-secret CLIENT_SECRET
agentcore deploy

aws bedrock-agentcore-control create-oauth2-credential-provider \
  --name oauth-credential-provider \
  --credential-provider-vendor CustomOAuth2 \
  --oauth2-provider-config-input '{
    "customOAuth2ProviderConfig": {
      "oauthDiscovery": { "discoveryUrl": "DISCOVERY_URL" },
      "clientId": "CLIENT_ID", "clientSecret": "CLIENT_SECRET" }}'

# API key
agentcore add credential --name api-key-credential-provider --type api-key --api-key API_KEY_VALUE
aws bedrock-agentcore-control create-api-key-credential-provider \
  --name api-key-credential-provider --api-key API_KEY_VALUE
Why this is the main event

Decoupled inbound and outbound auth is Gateway's biggest security contribution. A support agent authenticates a user with a Cognito JWT; the gateway calls Salesforce with a vault-held OAuth credential. The user's session never carries the Salesforce token, the agent's code never sees it, and rotation happens in the vault without redeploying anything.

17. Policy engine (Cedar) #

AgentCore Policy enforces business rules on tool calls at the gateway boundary, using the open-source Cedar language — deterministic authorization outside the agent's control, so no amount of prompt manipulation bypasses it.

bash
agentcore add policy-engine --name RefundPolicyEngine \
  --attach-to-gateways PolicyGateway --attach-mode ENFORCE

agentcore add policy --name RefundLimit \
  --engine RefundPolicyEngine --source refund_policy.cedar

# or generate Cedar from natural language
agentcore add policy --name RefundLimit --engine RefundPolicyEngine \
  --generate "Only allow refunds under 1000 dollars" --gateway PolicyGateway
text
permit(principal,
  action == AgentCore::Action::"RefundTarget___process_refund",
  resource == AgentCore::Gateway::"arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-east-1:123456789012:gateway/abc123")
when {
  context.input.amount < 1000
};
ModeBehaviour
ENFORCEEvery tool call evaluated. Default-deny: allowed only if a permit matches; any matching forbid wins regardless.
AUDITDecisions logged to CloudWatch without blocking — the correct way to roll out.

Notable properties: conditions can inspect tool arguments (context.input.amount), which is authorization no IAM policy can express; Cedar requires a specific gateway ARN (no wildcards), so deployment is two-phase — create the gateway, capture the ARN, then attach the policy; and since June 2026 Policy integrates Bedrock Guardrails to evaluate outputs for prompt injection, harmful content and sensitive-data exposure.

Roll out in AUDIT first

ENFORCE is default-deny. Attaching an engine with an incomplete policy set silently blocks every tool your agents use. Run in AUDIT, read the CloudWatch decisions, write permits until the deny log is empty, and only then switch.

18. Interceptors #

Lambda functions that run inside each gateway invocation, at one or both interception points.

PointRunsTypical use
REQUESTBefore the gateway calls the targetValidation, transformation, custom authorization, argument redaction
RESPONSEAfter the target responds, before returning to the callerResponse filtering, PII scrubbing, header injection, usage metering
json
"interceptorConfigurations": [
  {
    "interceptor": { "lambdaFunction": { "arn": "arn:aws:lambda:us-east-1:123456789012:function:GatewayReqInterceptor" } },
    "interceptionPoints": ["REQUEST"],
    "inputConfiguration": {
      "passRequestHeaders": false,
      "payloadFilter": { "exclude": [ { "jsonPath": "$.arguments.ssn" } ] }
    }
  }
]

Limits and rules worth knowing: at most one REQUEST and one RESPONSE interceptor per gateway; Lambda is currently the only implementation; passRequestHeaders defaults to false and should stay there unless you truly need headers, since they carry bearer tokens; and interceptors must be idempotent because gateways retry on failure or timeout.

Latency and blast radius

An interceptor is a synchronous Lambda on every single call — cold starts and all. It is also a single point of failure for the whole gateway. Prefer Cedar policy for authorization decisions (evaluated in-service, no Lambda) and reserve interceptors for things policy genuinely cannot express, such as payload transformation or external usage metering.

19. Rate limits #

Since August 2026 you can define your own rate limits on top of the service quotas, via CreateRateLimit, GetRateLimit, UpdateRateLimit, DeleteRateLimit, ListRateLimits and BatchPutRateLimitEntries.

MetricApplies toSemantics
Request rate (RPS / RPM)All target typesOne unit per request regardless of duration
Token rate (TPM)Inference targets onlyInput + output tokens; estimated upfront, reconciled after the response
Connections (CPS)Streaming workloadsA 100-second streaming call holds one connection slot for 100 seconds

Scoping dimensions

KeyBuckets traffic by
targetNameBackend — protects a fragile downstream service
toolNameIndividual tool — throttle the expensive one only
qualifiedModelIdModel — restrict costly models, or zero-rate them to block access
$.context.jwt.<claim>Identity claim — per-tier or per-user fairness
$.context.iam.principal / .sourceIdentityIAM principal or source identity

Customer-defined limits are evaluated first and cannot exceed the account-level service quotas. Best practice: always include a wildcard (*) catch-all entry so unmatched callers are still governed; avoid high-cardinality JWT claims as dimensions; and treat rate limiting as traffic management rather than security, since it is fail-open.

A neat trick

A zero-rate entry on qualifiedModelId is an access-control mechanism: it blocks a model for a user tier outright, without touching IAM or writing a Cedar policy.

20. IAM reference #

20.1 Gateway service role — trust policy

json
{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [{
    "Sid": "AllowBedrockAgentCoreAssumeRole",
    "Effect": "Allow",
    "Principal": { "Service": "bedrock-agentcore.amazonaws.com" },
    "Action": "sts:AssumeRole",
    "Condition": {
      "StringEquals": { "aws:SourceAccount": "123456789012" },
      "ArnLike": { "aws:SourceArn": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:us-east-1:123456789012:*" }
    }
  }]
}

21. CreateGateway reference #

json
POST /gateways/ HTTP/1.1

{
  "name": "string",                                  // ([0-9a-zA-Z][-]?){1,48}, required
  "description": "string",
  "clientToken": "string",
  "roleArn": "string",                               // required
  "protocolType": "MCP",
  "protocolConfiguration": {
    "mcpConfiguration": {
      "searchType": "string",                        // enables x_amz_bedrock_agentcore_search
      "supportedVersions": ["2026-07-28", "2025-11-25"],
      "instructions": "string"                       // shown to MCP clients
    }
  },
  "authorizerType": "CUSTOM_JWT | AWS_IAM | NONE | AUTHENTICATE_ONLY",   // required
  "authorizerConfiguration": {
    "customJwtConfiguration": { "audience": ["string"], "issuer": "string" }
  },
  "kmsKeyArn": "string",
  "exceptionLevel": "DEBUG",                         // verbose errors for debugging
  "policyEngineConfiguration": { "arn": "string", "mode": "string" },
  "interceptorConfigurations": [
    {
      "interceptor": { "lambdaFunction": { "arn": "string" } },
      "interceptionPoints": ["REQUEST", "RESPONSE"],
      "inputConfiguration": {
        "passRequestHeaders": true,
        "payloadFilter": { "exclude": [ { "jsonPath": "string" } ] }
      }
    }
  ],
  "tags": { "key": "value" }
}

Returns HTTP 202 with:

json
{
  "gatewayId": "string",
  "gatewayArn": "arn:aws:bedrock-agentcore:REGION:ACCOUNT:gateway/GATEWAY_ID",
  "gatewayUrl": "https://GATEWAY_ID.gateway.bedrock-agentcore.REGION.amazonaws.com",
  "status": "CREATING | UPDATING | UPDATE_UNSUCCESSFUL | DELETING | READY | FAILED",
  "statusReasons": ["string"],
  "workloadIdentityDetails": { "workloadIdentityArn": "string" },
  "customTransformConfiguration": { "lambda": { "arn": "string" } },
  "wafConfiguration": { "failureMode": "string" },
  "webAclArn": "string",
  "createdAt": "timestamp", "updatedAt": "timestamp"
}
Also available

webAclArn and wafConfiguration attach an AWS WAF web ACL to the gateway — useful for IP reputation, geo restrictions and managed rule groups on top of the authorizer. exceptionLevel: "DEBUG" returns verbose target errors; leave it off in production, since backend error text can leak internals to callers.

22. Quickstart #

22.1 Prerequisites

  • Node.js 20+, Python 3.10+, AWS credentials, and model access enabled in Bedrock.
  • npm install -g @aws/agentcore — the AgentCore CLI (the Python starter toolkit is legacy).
  • IAM permissions for bedrock-agentcore, Lambda and role creation.

22.2 Create a gateway

bash
# Development: no inbound auth
agentcore add gateway --name TestGateway --authorizer-type NONE --runtimes MyGatewayAgent

# Production: JWT
agentcore add gateway --name SecureGateway \
  --authorizer-type CUSTOM_JWT \
  --discovery-url https://cognito-idp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/POOL_ID/.well-known/openid-configuration \
  --allowed-audience CLIENT_ID \
  --runtimes MyGatewayAgent
python
import boto3

ctl = boto3.client("bedrock-agentcore-control", region_name="us-east-1")

resp = ctl.create_gateway(
    name="SecureGateway",
    description="Order and support tools",
    roleArn="arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/GatewayServiceRole",
    protocolType="MCP",
    protocolConfiguration={
        "mcpConfiguration": {
            "searchType": "SEMANTIC",                  # cannot be added later
            "supportedVersions": ["2026-07-28", "2025-11-25"],
        }
    },
    authorizerType="CUSTOM_JWT",
    authorizerConfiguration={
        "customJwtConfiguration": {
            "issuer": "https://cognito-idp.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/us-east-1_ABC123",
            "audience": ["3n4b5c6d7e8f9g0h1i2j3k4l5m"],
        }
    },
)
print(resp["gatewayUrl"])

22.3 Add a target

bash
agentcore add gateway-target --name orders \
  --type lambda-function-arn \
  --lambda-arn arn:aws:lambda:us-east-1:123456789012:function:OrdersFunction \
  --tool-schema-file tools.json \
  --gateway SecureGateway

agentcore deploy
agentcore status
python
ctl.create_gateway_target(
    gatewayIdentifier=gateway_id,
    name="orders",
    targetConfiguration={
        "mcp": {
            "lambda": {
                "lambdaArn": "arn:aws:lambda:us-east-1:123456789012:function:OrdersFunction",
                "toolSchema": {"inlinePayload": [
                    {"name": "get_order",
                     "description": "Look up an order by its ID",
                     "inputSchema": {
                         "type": "object",
                         "properties": {"order_id": {"type": "string",
                                                     "description": "Order identifier, e.g. #35476"}},
                         "required": ["order_id"]}}
                ]}
            }
        }
    },
    credentialProviderConfigurations=[{"credentialProviderType": "GATEWAY_IAM_ROLE"}],
)

22.4 Call it

bash
curl -X POST "$GATEWAY_URL/mcp" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $ACCESS_TOKEN" \
  -H "MCP-Protocol-Version: 2025-11-25" \
  -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/list","params":{}}'

bash
agentcore remove gateway --name SecureGateway
agentcore deploy

23. Client integration #

Because the gateway is an MCP server, any MCP-capable framework connects without custom code — Strands, LangGraph, LangChain, CrewAI, LlamaIndex.

python
from strands import Agent
from strands.models import BedrockModel
from strands.tools.mcp.mcp_client import MCPClient
from mcp.client.streamable_http import streamablehttp_client

GATEWAY_URL = "https://GATEWAY_ID.gateway.bedrock-agentcore.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/mcp"

def transport():
    return streamablehttp_client(GATEWAY_URL, headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {TOKEN}"})

def all_tools(client):
    """Large catalogues paginate — collect every page."""
    tools, token, more = [], None, True
    while more:
        page = client.list_tools_sync(pagination_token=token)
        tools.extend(page)
        token = page.pagination_token
        more = token is not None
    return tools

mcp_client = MCPClient(transport)
with mcp_client:
    agent = Agent(
        model=BedrockModel(model_id="anthropic.claude-3-7-sonnet-20250219-v1:0", streaming=True),
        tools=all_tools(mcp_client),
    )
    print(agent("What's the status of order #35476?"))
Don't blindly load every tool

The snippet above is the standard example, and it is exactly what you should not do once the catalogue is large — every tool consumes context and adds a wrong path. Beyond a few dozen tools, search first and hand the agent only the relevant subset.

24. Observability #

24.1 Metrics

Namespace AWS/Bedrock-AgentCore, batched at one-minute intervals.

MetricMeasuresStatistics
InvocationsAll data-plane requests, any statusSum
Throttles429 responsesSum
SystemErrors / UserErrors5xx / 4xx excluding 429Sum
LatencyRequest receipt → first response token (ms)Avg, Min, Max, p50, p90, p99
DurationTotal end-to-end processing (ms)Avg, Min, Max, p50, p90, p99
TargetExecutionTimeTime spent executing the backend (ms)Avg, Min, Max, p50, p90, p99
TargetTypeRequests by target type (MCP, Lambda, OpenAPI…)Sum

Dimensions: Operation, Protocol, Method (e.g. tools/list), Resource (gateway ARN), Name (tool name).

The one comparison worth graphing

Duration minus TargetExecutionTime is gateway overhead — authorizer, policy, interceptors, translation. If that gap grows, the problem is your configuration, not the backend. Split by Name to find the tool that's actually slow.

Logged content includes request start/completion, target configuration errors, MCP authorization header problems, malformed MCP parameters, and request/response bodies.

bash
aws cloudwatch put-metric-alarm \
  --alarm-name "GatewayHighErrorRate" \

25.1 Resources

QuotaDefaultAdjustable

Tool name length256 charactersyes

25.2 Invocation & schema

QuotaDefaultAdjustable
Gateway invocation timeout15 minutesyes
Inline schema size1 MByes
S3 schema payload size10 MByes
tool-call / tool-list / tool-search payload6 MByes

25.3 Rates

OperationRateAdjustable
tool-call / tool-list (gateway and account level)200 TPS eachyes
Concurrent connections (gateway and account level)5,000 eachyes
Search-based tool-call25 per minuteyes
Web Search Tool requests10 TPSyes
CreateGateway / UpdateGateway / DeleteGateway5 TPS eachyes
GetGateway / ListGateways10 TPS eachyes
CreateGatewayTarget / UpdateGatewayTarget / DeleteGatewayTarget5 TPS eachyes
GetGatewayTarget / ListGatewayTargets10 TPS eachyes
Concurrent target mutations on one gateway5yes
Read that search line again

25 transactions per minute — about 0.4 TPS, against 200 TPS for ordinary tool calls. If every agent turn starts with a semantic search, you will throttle at roughly 25 concurrent turns per minute across the whole account. Search once per task, cache the resulting tool subset for the session, and request an increase before you scale.

26. Pricing & cost model #

Verify before you budget

Rates below are published US on-demand rates as of 18 Aug 2026 and vary by region.

DimensionRateNotes
API invocations (ListTools, InvokeTool, Ping)$0.005 per 1,000The everyday cost — effectively negligible
Search API$0.025 per 1,0005× a normal invocation
Tool indexing$0.02 per 100 tools / monthStanding cost of a large catalogue
Web Search Tool$7.00 per 1,000 queries~1,400× a normal tool invocation
Policy authorization$0.000025 per requestFirst 100 temporal policies per engine incur no authorization charge
Natural-language policy authoring$0.13 per 1,000 tokensOne-off, at authoring time
AgentCore IdentityFree via Gateway$0.010 per 1,000 token/API-key requests only for standalone use

26.1 Worked example

500 tools indexed, 10 M tool invocations per month, one semantic search per task across 1 M tasks, Policy in ENFORCE:

text
Tool invocations : 10,000,000 / 1,000 × $0.005     = $ 50.00
Search           :  1,000,000 / 1,000 × $0.025     = $ 25.00
Indexing         :        500 / 100   × $0.02      = $  0.10
Policy           : 10,000,000 × $0.000025          = $250.00
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Gateway total (excluding backend and model costs)  ≈ $325/month

… add ONE Web Search per task:
                   1,000,000 / 1,000 × $7.00       = $7,000.00   ← 20× everything else

Three observations. Gateway itself is cheap — at ten million calls the invocation line is $50, so the hop is not what costs money. Policy is the quiet multiplier, at 5× the invocation cost when enforced on every call; still worth it, but budget for it. And Web Search dominates everything the moment it enters a per-task loop, which is precisely why it deserves a rate limit and a policy guard.

26.2 Cost controls

  • Search for discovery, not dispatch — the quota and the price agree.
  • Rate-limit or zero-rate Web Search per user tier.
  • Prune unused tools; indexing is small but the context cost of a bloated catalogue is not.
  • Tag gateways for cost allocation and split by team or environment.
  • Watch Invocations against expected traffic — a retry storm inside an agent loop shows up here first.

27. Troubleshooting #

SymptomLikely causeFix
Lambda returns “unknown tool” for every callTool name arrives prefixed as target___toolStrip on the ___ delimiter before routing (§6.2)
OpenAPI target fails to importSwagger 2.0, missing operationId, or oneOf/anyOf/allOfConvert to OpenAPI 3.x, add operationId everywhere, flatten composed schemas
x_amz_bedrock_agentcore_search not foundSemantic search wasn't enabled at creationNot retrofittable — create a new gateway with searchType set and repoint clients
Search throttles under light load25 requests per minute, not per secondSearch once per task; cache the tool subset; request a quota increase
MCP target's new tools don't appearCatalogue is cached under DEFAULT listing modeCall SynchronizeGatewayTargets; poll GetGatewayTarget until complete
SynchronizeGatewayTargets rejectedTarget was created with a static mcpToolSchemaRemove the static schema first, then sync
Semantic search or 3LO unavailable on a targetTarget uses DYNAMIC listing modeSwitch to DEFAULT — the two features require a pre-indexed catalogue
Every tool call denied after attaching a policyENFORCE mode is default-denySwitch to AUDIT, read the decision logs, add permit policies, then re-enforce
Cedar policy rejected at creationWildcard resource, or gateway ARN not yet knownCedar requires a concrete gateway ARN — deploy the gateway first, then attach the policy
401 with WWW-AuthenticateMissing/expired bearer token on a CUSTOM_JWT gatewayRefresh the token; verify issuer and audience match the authorizer config
403 on a SigV4 gatewayCaller lacks bedrock-agentcore:InvokeGatewayAdd the action scoped to the gateway ARN
Outbound calls unauthorizedService role missing GetResourceOauth2Token or secretsmanager:GetSecretValueAdd the token-vault and secret permissions (§20.2)
3LO consent link failsAuthorization URL and session URI expire after 10 minutes and are bound to the initiatorRe-initiate the flow; complete it in the same session — links cannot be shared
SigV4 to an MCP server failsBackend is behind an ALB or a raw EC2 endpointSigV4 works only with Gateway, Runtime, API Gateway and Lambda Function URLs — use OAuth or API key
Latency jumped after a config changeAn interceptor Lambda is now in the hot pathCompare Duration vs TargetExecutionTime; move authorization into Cedar where possible
Inference requests hit the wrong providerUnqualified model ID matched multiple targetsAlways use {targetName}/{modelId}
Agent picks the wrong toolWeak description fields, or too many tools in contextRewrite descriptions to say when to use each tool; narrow the catalogue per agent

27.1 Debug checklist

bash
# 1. Is the gateway READY, and what does it actually expose?
aws bedrock-agentcore-control get-gateway --gateway-identifier "$GATEWAY_ID"
aws bedrock-agentcore-control list-gateway-targets --gateway-identifier "$GATEWAY_ID"

# 2. What tools does an MCP client really see? (exact names, prefixes and all)
curl -s -X POST "$GATEWAY_URL/mcp" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/list","params":{}}' | jq '.result.tools[].name'

# 3. Call one tool directly, bypassing the agent
curl -s -X POST "$GATEWAY_URL/mcp" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -d '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":2,"method":"tools/call",
       "params":{"name":"orders___get_order","arguments":{"order_id":"35476"}}}'

# 4. What did the gateway log?
aws logs tail "/aws/vendedlogs/bedrock-agentcore/gateway/APPLICATION_LOGS/$GATEWAY_ID" --follow

# 5. Where is the time going?

Appendix C — Glossary #

TermMeaning
GatewayManaged MCP/HTTP/inference endpoint owning inbound auth, policy and observability.
TargetA backend registered on a gateway, in the MCP, HTTP or inference category.

2LO / 3LOTwo-legged (client credentials, service-to-service) and three-legged (authorization code, on behalf of a user) OAuth.
Token exchangeTrading a validated inbound token for a downstream token scoped to an audience.

Policy engineCedar authorization attached to a gateway in ENFORCE or AUDIT mode.
